0002d8f7     
0002d8fd Fill the output tensor with int32 samples from uniform distribution [`min`, `max`].
0002d952 - The range can be defined either by arguments or input blobs. `min` and `max` are inclusive.
0002d9b0     - If the range is given by input blobs, you also need to give the shape as input.
0002da06     - When the range is given as arguments, this operator enforces min <= max. When the range is given as inputs, the constraint is not enforced.
0002da98     - When the range is given as inputs and max < min, the first dimension of the output is set to 0. This behavior is allowed so that dynamically sampling indices into a dynamically sized tensor is possible.
0002db69 - The shape of the output can be given as argument or input.
0002dba7 Github Links:

0002dc01 - https://github.com/caffe2/caffe2/blob/master/caffe2/operators/filler_op.cc

0002e84c     
0002e852   Convert historical features + labels and candidate features + prod_predictions
0002e8a3   to mobile ranking format
0002e8c2   Convert the batch sequence features into training examples
0002e903   Convert example based row features to sequence based
0002e93a   row features to accommodate mobile ranking
0002e96b   Conver from mobile ranking format to historical features + labels and
0002e9b3   candidate features + prod_predictions format. Operator assumes that all
0002e9fd   history features are the same
0002ea21 The transposed convolution consumes an input vector, the filter blob, and
0002ea6b the bias blob, and computes the output. Note that other parameters, such as
0002eab7 the stride and kernel size, or the pads' sizes in each direction are not
0002eb00 necessary for input because they are provided by the
0002eb35 ConvTransposeUnpoolOpBase operator. Various dimension checks are done
0002eb7b implicitly, and the sizes are specified in the Input docs for this operator.
0002ebc8 As is expected, the filter is deconvolved with a subset of the
0002ec07 image and the bias is added; this is done throughout the image data and the
0002ec53 output is computed. As a side note on the implementation layout:
0002ec94 conv_transpose_op_impl.h is the templated implementation of the
0002ecd4 conv_transpose_op.h file, which is why they are separate files.
0002ed18 PadImage pads values around the boundary of an image according to the pad
0002ed62 values and stride sizes defined by the ConvPoolOpBase operator.
0002eda6 The ConvTranspose op takes an input data tensor $X$, an input weight tensor $filter$, and optionally an input bias tensor $bias$. It then computes the transposed convolution, sometimes referred to as deconvolution, and produces a single output tensor $Y$. The hyperparameters of the op such as kernel size, stride, and padding are specified as args. At each stride, the filter is deconvolved with a subset of $X$ and the $bias$ is added. This is done throughout the input data until the output computation is complete.
0002efae The output shapes are computed as follows. The number of channels in the output feature map is the number of kernels specified in the filter blob. The spatial height and width are computed as:
0002f070 $$H_{out} = (H_{in}-1)*strides[0] - 2*pads[0] + kernels[0]$$
0002f0af $$W_{out} = (W_{in}-1)*strides[1] - 2*pads[1] + kernels[1]$$
0002f0ed Note on the implementation layout: conv_transpose_op_impl.h is the templated implementation of the conv_transpose_op.h file, which is why they are separate files. Also, in the implementation this operator inherits from the *ConvTransposeUnpoolOpBase* operator.
0002f1f3 Github Links:
0002f201 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/tree/main/caffe2/operators/conv_transpose_op.h
0002f255 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/tree/main/caffe2/operators/conv_transpose_op.cc
0002f2aa - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/tree/main/caffe2/operators/conv_transpose_unpool_op_base.h

0002fdc5 Produces a slice of the input Int8 tensor. Currently, only slicing in a single
0002fe14 dimension is supported.
0002fe2c Slices are passed as 2 1D vectors or as two keyword argument lists with starting
0002fe7d and end indices for each dimension of the input `data` tensor. If a negative
0002feca value is passed for any of the start or end indices, it represents the number of
0002ff1b elements before the end of that dimension. End indices are non-inclusive unless
0002ff6b negative (end index -1 means up to and including the last element).

00030023       [2, 3],
00030031       [6, 7],

000300db sampling. Using the second input will require you to provide shape via the first
0003012c input.
00030135 Merge the probabilites and bounding boxes of the raw concepts to those of
0003017f the merged concepts.
00030196 Computes the result of passing an input vector X into a fully
000301d4 connected layer with 2D weight matrix W and 1D bias vector b. That is,
0003021b the layer computes Y = X * W^T + b, where X has size (M x K),
00030259 W has size (N x K), b has size (N), and Y has size (M x N),
00030295 where M is often the batch size.
000302b8 NOTE: X does not need to explicitly be a 2D vector; rather, it will be
000302ff coerced into one. For an arbitrary n-dimensional tensor
00030337 X \in [a_0, a_1 * ... * a_{n-1}]. Only this case is supported!
00030376 Lastly, even though b is a 1D vector of size N, it is copied/resized to
000303be be size (M x N) implicitly and added to each vector in the batch.
00030400 Each of these dimensions must be matched correctly, or else the operator
00030449 will throw errors.
0003045e The operator computes the softmax normalized values for each layer in the batch
000304ae  of the given input. The input is a 2-D tensor (Tensor<float>) of size
000304f5 (batch_size x input_feature_dimensions). The output tensor has the same shape
00030543 and contains the softmax normalized values of the corresponding input.
0003058b X does not need to explicitly be a 2D vector; rather, it will be
000305cc coerced into one. For an arbitrary n-dimensional tensor
00030604 X \in [a_0, a_1, ..., a_{k-1}, a_k, ..., a_{n-1}] and k is
0003063f the axis provided, then X will be coerced into a 2-dimensional tensor with
0003068a dimensions [a_0 * ... * a_{k-1}, a_k * ... * a_{n-1}]. For the default
000306d1 case where axis=1, this means the X tensor will be coerced into a 2D tensor
0003071d of dimensions [a_0, a_1 * ... * a_{n-1}], where a_0 is often the batch size.
0003076a In this situation, we must have a_0 = N and a_1 * ... * a_{n-1} = D.
000307af Each of these dimensions must be matched correctly, or else the operator
000307f8 will throw errors.
0003080d Generate bounding box proposals for Faster RCNN. The proposals are generated for
0003085e a list of images based on image score 'score', bounding box regression result
000308ac 'deltas' as well as predefined bounding box shapes 'anchors'. Greedy
000308f1 non-maximum suppression is applied to generate the final bounding boxes.
0003093c Apply NMS to each class (except background) and limit the number of
00030980 returned boxes.
00030992 Transform proposal bounding boxes to target bounding box using bounding box
000309de     regression deltas.
000309f7 Reshape the input tensor similar to numpy.reshape.
00030a2b It takes a tensor as input and an optional tensor specifying the new shape.
00030a77 When the second input is absent, an extra argument `shape` must be specified.
00030ac5 It outputs the reshaped tensor as well as the original shape.
00030b04 At most one dimension of the new shape can be -1. In this case, the value is
00030b51 inferred from the size of the tensor and the remaining dimensions. A dimension
00030ba0 could also be 0, in which case the actual dimension value is going to be copied
00030bf0 from the input tensor.
00030c09   ROI warping for Mask R-CNN, more close to RoIAlign described in the paper.
00030c58     Creates quantized tensor of type int32 with scale and zero point info.
00030ca5     Creates quantized tensor of type char(byte) with scale and zero point info.
00030cf7 ReluGradient takes both Y and dY and uses this to update dX according to the
00030d44 chain rule and derivatives of the rectified linear function.
00030d83 EluGradient takes both Y and dY and uses this to update dX according to the

00031a9b Transpose the input tensor by permuting the axes of the input according

00031c97 Resizes the spatial dimensions of the input using nearest neighbor
00031cda interpolation. The `width_scale` and `height_scale` arguments
00031d18 control the size of the output, which is given by:
00031d4b output_width = floor(input_width * width_scale)
00031d7b output_height = floor(output_height * height_scale)
00031db0 Error from operator: 
00031dc8 Applies spatial batch normalization to the input tensor as described in the original paper, [Batch Normalization: Accelerating Deep Network Training by Reducing Internal Covariate Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.03167). Be aware, this operator has two different output sets, depending on the value of *is_test*. According to the paper, the primary operation of spatial batch normalization is:
00031f54 $$Y = \frac{X - \mu_x}{\sqrt{\sigma^2_{x} + \epsilon}}*\gamma + b$$
00031f99 In the equation, $\mu_x$ is the *mean*, $X$ is the input data, $\sigma^2_{x}$ is the *var*, $\epsilon$ is *epsilon*, $\gamma$ is the *scale*, $b$ is the *bias*, and $Y$ is the output data. The *momentum* arg also affects this calculation in the computation of the running mean and variance. The influence of *momentum* is as follows:
000320e8 $$running\_mean = running\_mean * momentum + mean * (1 - momentum)$$
0003212e $$running\_var = running\_var * momentum + var * (1 - momentum)$$
00032171 Output when is_test = 0 (train mode): *Y, mean, var, saved_mean, saved_var*
000321be Output when is_test = 1 (test mode): *Y*
000321e8 Github Links:
000321f6 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/spatial_batch_norm_op.cc
0003224f - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/spatial_batch_norm_op.h
000322ab `Dropout` takes one input data tensor (`X`) and produces two tensor outputs, `Y` and
00032300 `mask`. If the `is_test` argument is zero (default=0), the output `Y` will be the input
00032358 with random elements zeroed. The probability that a given element is zeroed is
000323a7 determined by the `ratio` argument.
000323cc If the `is_test` argument is set to non-zero, the output `Y` is exactly the same as the
00032424 input `X`. Note that outputs are scaled by a factor of $\frac{1}{1-ratio}$ during
00032476 training, so that during test time, we can simply compute an identity function. This
000324cb scaling is important because we want the output at test time to equal the expected value
00032524 at training time. Dropout has been proven to be an effective regularization technique to
0003257d prevent overfitting during training.
000325a4 Github Links:
000325b3 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/dropout_op.h
00032600 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/dropout_op.cc

00033f72 Reshape the input tensor similar to numpy's
00033f9e [reshape](https://docs.scipy.org/doc/numpy/reference/generated/numpy.reshape.html).
00033ff3 Takes a tensor as input and an optional tensor specifying the new shape. When
00034041 the second input is absent, an extra argument shape must be specified. Outputs
00034090 the reshaped tensor as well as the original shape.
000340c4 At most one dimension of the new shape can be -1. In this case, the value is
00034111 inferred from the size of the tensor and the remaining dimensions. A dimension
00034160 could also be 0, in which case the actual dimension value is going to be copied
000341b0 from the input tensor.
000341c8 For empty tensor, we will set the -1 dimension to be 0 (if one dimension is -1).
00034219 When the tensor is empty, dimension of 0 will remain to be 0.
00034257 E.g: data=np.empty(shape=[4, 0]), shape=[0, -1], the output tensor will be
000342a2 np.emtpy(shape=[0, 0])
000342ba Github Links:
000342c9 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/reshape_op.cc

00034d3a The *InstanceNorm* op applies Instance Normalization over a 4D input as described in [Instance Normalization: The Missing Ingredient for Fast Stylization](https://arxiv.org/abs/1607.08022).
00034df9 $$output = \frac{input-\mu_{input}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{input}^2} + \epsilon}*scale + bias$$
00034e51 Notice, two of the outputs are optional so there are three output cases for this op. Case 1: output; Case 2: output, saved_mean; Case 3: output, saved_mean, saved_inv_stdev.
00034f00 Github Links:
00034f0f - https://github.com/caffe2/caffe2/blob/master/caffe2/operators/instance_norm_op.h
00034f62 - https://github.com/caffe2/caffe2/blob/master/caffe2/operators/instance_norm_op.cc

00035487 The *Gather* op accepts a *DATA* tensor of rank $r >= 1$ and *INDICES* tensor of rank $q$ as inputs. It then gathers entries of the outer-most dimension of *DATA*, indexed by *INDICES*, and concatenate them in an output tensor of rank $q + (r - 1)$.

00035c86 Fill the output tensor with float samples from uniform distribution [`min`, `max`].
00035cdb - The range can be defined either by arguments or input blobs. `min` and `max` are inclusive.
00035d39     - If the range is given by input blobs, you also need to give the shape as input.
00035d8f     - When the range is given as arguments, this operator enforces min <= max. When the range is given as inputs, the constraint is not enforced.
00035e21     - When the range is given as inputs and max < min, the first dimension of the output is set to 0. This behavior is allowed so that dynamically sampling indices into a dynamically sized tensor is possible.
00035ef2 - The shape of the output can be given as argument or input.
00035f30 Github Links:
00035f3e - https://github.com/caffe2/caffe2/blob/master/caffe2/operators/filler_op.h
00035f8a - https://github.com/caffe2/caffe2/blob/master/caffe2/operators/filler_op.cc

00036dba The FC operator computes an output $(Y)$ as a linear combination of the input data blob $(X)$ with a weight blob $(W)$ and bias blob $(b)$. More formally,
00036e56 $$Y = XW^T+b$$
00036e66 Here, $X$ is a matrix of shape $(M,K)$, $W$ is a matrix of shape $(N,K)$, $b$ is a vector of length $N$, and $Y$ is a matrix of shape $(M,N)$. $N$ can be thought of as the number of nodes in the layer, $M$ is the batch size, and $K$ is the number of features in an input observation.
00036f83 *NOTE: $X$ does not need to explicitly be a 2-dimensional matrix, however, if it is not it will be coerced into one. For an arbitrary $n$-dimensional tensor $X$, e.g. $[a_0, a_1, \ldots ,a_{k-1}, a_k, \ldots , a_{n-1}]$, where $a_i$ in $N$, and $k$ is the $axis$ arg provided, then $X$ will be coerced into a 2-dimensional tensor with dimensions $[a_0 * \ldots * a_{k-1}, a_k * \ldots * a_{n-1}]$. For the default case where axis=1, this means the $X$ tensor will be coerced into a 2D tensor of dimensions $[a_0, a_1 * \ldots * a_{n-1}]$, where $a_0$ is often the batch size. In this situation, we must have $a_0 = M$ and $a_1 * \ldots * a_{n-1} = K$. Lastly, even though $b$ is a vector of length $N$, it is copied and resized to shape $(M x N)$ implicitly, then added to each vector in the batch.*
000372a4 Github Links:
000372b2 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/fully_connected_op.h
00037307 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/fully_connected_op.cc
0003735e <details>
00037369 <summary> <b>Example</b> </summary>
0003738e **Code**
0003739d // In this example, our batch size is 1 (M=1), the input observation will have
000373ec //   6 features (K=6), and the layer will have one hidden node (N=1). The
00037436 //   expected output is Y=7.
00037453 workspace.ResetWorkspace()
0003746f op = core.CreateOperator(
00037489     "FC",
00037493     ["X", "W", "b"],
000374a8     ["Y"]
000374b5 // Create X: MxK
000374c6 data = np.array([1,2,3,4,5,6]).astype(np.float32)
000374f8 data = data[np.newaxis,:]
00037513 // Create W: NxK
00037524 weights = np.array(np.array([1,1/2.,1/3.,1/4.,1/5.,1/6.])).astype(np.float32)
00037572 weights = weights[np.newaxis,:]
00037593 // Create b: N
000375a2 bias = np.array([1.]).astype(np.float32)
000375cc // Put the inputs into the workspace
000375f1 workspace.FeedBlob("X", data)
0003760f workspace.FeedBlob("W", weights)
00037630 workspace.FeedBlob("b", bias)
0003764f // Run the operator
00037663 workspace.RunOperatorOnce(op)
00037681 print("Y:\n", workspace.FetchBlob("Y"))
000376af **Result**
000376c3  [[7.]]
000376d1 </details>
000376df Element-wise sum of each of the input tensors. The first input tensor can be used
00037731 in-place as the output tensor, in which case the sum will be done in place and
00037780 results will be accumulated the first input tensor. All inputs and outputs must
000377d0 have the same shape and data type.

00037ce3 Calculates the hyperbolic tangent of the given input tensor element-wise. This
00037d32 operation can be done in an in-place fashion too, by providing the same input
00037d80 and output blobs.
00037d93 Github Links:

00037e64     "Tanh",

00038060 This op fills an output tensor with samples drawn from a normal distribution specified by the mean and standard deviation arguments. 00038948 This op fills an output tensor with values sampled from a uniform distribution with the range determined by the desired shape of the output. Rather, than specifying the range of values manually, the novelty of Xavier Fill is that it automatically scales the range of the distribution it draws from based on the size of the desired output tensor. For more information check out the paper [Understanding the difficulty of training deep feedforward neural networks](http://proceedings.mlr.press/v9/glorot10a/glorot10a.pdf). The output tensor shape is specified by the *shape* argument. However, if *input_as_shape* is set to *true*, then the *input* should be a 1D tensor containing the desired output shape (the dimensions specified in *extra_shape* will also be appended). In this case, the *shape* argument should **not** be set.
00038c87 *Note: Do not set the shape argument and pass in an input at the same time.*
00038cd5 Github Links:

00038d2f - https://github.com/caffe2/caffe2/blob/master/caffe2/operators/filler_op.cc

00038f1f This op implements the exponential linear unit (ELU) activation function as described in [Fast and Accurate Deep Network Learning by Exponential Linear Units (ELUs)](https://arxiv.org/abs/1511.07289). The op takes an input tensor $X$ of arbitrary shape, computes the elementwise elu operation, and returns a vector $Y$ of the same shape as output. The alpha parameter may be passed as an argument, but defaults to 1. The elu operation is defined as
000390e1 $$y=f(x) =\begin{cases}\alpha(e^x-1) & x < 0 \\ x & otherwise\end{cases}$$
0003912d Github Links:

00039f15 This op fills a uint8 output tensor with the data specified by the *value* argument. The data must previously be serialized as a byte string. If *input_as_shape* is set to *true*, then the *input* should be a 1D tensor containing the desired output shape (the dimensions specified in *extra_shape* will also be appended). In this case, the *shape* argument should **not** be set.

0003abdb Input `data` is a N * D matrix. Apply box-cox transform for each column.
0003ac24 `lambda1` and `lambda2` is of size D that defines the hyper-parameters for
0003ac6f the transform of each column `x` of the input `data`:
0003aca6     ln(x + lambda2), if lambda1 == 0
0003accb     ((x + lambda2)^lambda1 - 1)/lambda1, if lambda1 != 0

0003b203 The *PRelu* op takes input data tensor $X$, an input slope tensor $slope$, and produces one output tensor $Y$ of the same shape as $X.$ The op performs the element wise *PRelu* operation, defined as
0003b2cb $$y=prelu(x) =\begin{cases}slope * x & x < 0\\x & otherwise\end{cases}$$
0003b315 Note, is slope is size 1, the value is shared across the channels, otherwise $X$ and $slope$ must be the same shape. See [Delving Deep into Rectifiers: Surpassing Human-Level Performance on ImageNet Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.01852) for more information.
0003b425 Github Links:

0003c355 Applies the Softmax function to an n-dimensional input Tensor rescaling them so
0003c3a5 that the elements of the n-dimensional output Tensor lie in the range (0,1) and
0003c3f5 sum to 1. The softmax operator is typically the last layer in a classifier network,
0003c449 as its output can be interpreted as confidence probabilities of an input belonging
0003c49c to each class. The input is a 2-D tensor (Tensor) of size (batch_size x
0003c4e4 input_feature_dimensions). The output tensor has the same shape and contains the
0003c535 softmax normalized values of the corresponding input. The softmax function is
0003c583 defined as follows:
0003c598 $$softmax(x_i) = \frac{\exp(x_i)}{\sum_{j} \exp(x_j)}$$
0003c5d1 The input does not need to explicitly be a 2D vector; rather, it will be coerced
0003c622 into one. For an arbitrary n-dimensional tensor `X` in
0003c659 $[a_0, a_1, ..., a_{k-1}, a_k, ..., a_{n-1}]$, where k is the `axis` provided,
0003c6a8 then `X` will be coerced into a 2-dimensional tensor with dimensions
0003c6ed $[(a_0 * ... * a_{k-1}), (a_k * ... * a_{n-1})]$. For the default case where
0003c73a `axis`=1, the `X` tensor will be coerced into a 2D tensor of dimensions
0003c782 $[a_0, (a_1 * ... * a_{n-1})]$, where $a_0$ is often the batch size. In this
0003c7cf situation, we must have $a_0 = N$ and $a_1 * ... * a_{n-1} = D$. Each of these
0003c81e dimensions must be matched correctly, or else the operator will throw errors.
0003c86d Github Links:
0003c87c - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/softmax_op.h
0003c8c9 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/softmax_op.cc
0003c919 <details>
0003c924 <summary> <b>Example</b> </summary>
0003c949 **Code**
0003c957 workspace.ResetWorkspace()
0003c973 op = core.CreateOperator(
0003c98d     "Softmax",
0003c99c     ["X"],
0003c9a7     ["Y"]
0003c9b4 workspace.FeedBlob("X", np.random.randn(1, 5).astype(np.float32))
0003c9f6 print("input:", workspace.FetchBlob("X"))
0003ca20 workspace.RunOperatorOnce(op)
0003ca3e print("softmax:", workspace.FetchBlob("Y"))
0003ca70 **Result**

0003dc06 NSt6__ndk110__function6__funcIZN6caffe216ConvDocGeneratorEPKcE3$_0NS_9allocatorIS5_EEFvRNS2_8OpSchemaEEEE
0003dc70 NSt6__ndk110__function6__baseIFvRN6caffe28OpSchemaEEEE
0003dca8 The Conv2D operator computes a 2D convolution operation over an input blob $(X)$, with a filter blob $(filter)$ and a bias blob $(bias)$, and outputs a single output blob $(Y)$. Although there are several options for order, the convention is that the input $(X)$ is a blob of shape $(N,C_{in},H_{in},W_{in})$ and the output $(Y)$ is a blob of shape $(N,C_{out},H_{out},W_{out})$. Here, $N$ is the batch size, $C$ is the number of channels, $H$ is the spatial height, and $W$ is the spatial width. For example, if your input data was a batch of five, 100x120pixel RGB images, $X$ would have shape $(5,3,120,100)$.
0003df0e The $filter$ input blob may contain multiple filters and has shape $(M, C_{in}, K_H, K_W)$. Here, $M$ is the number of individual filters contained in the blob, $C_{in}$ is the number of channels of each filter (by convention in 2D convolution it is the same as the number of channels in the input), $K_H$ is the spatial height of the kernel, and $K_W$ is the spatial width of the kernel. The $bias$ blob is a vector of length $M$, where there is one bias for each filter in the $filter$ blob.
0003e0fd Given the shape of the input blob and the filter blob, we can calculate the shape of the output blob as follows. The number of items in the batch $N$ will stay the same. The number of channels in the output will equal the number of kernels in the filter blob, so $C_{out} = M.$ With stride and pad defined below, the spatial height and width of the output ($H_{out}$ and $W_{out}$) are calculated as
0003e28e $$H_{out} = \left \lfloor{\frac{H_{in} - K_H + 2*pad}{stride}+1}\right \rfloor$$
0003e2e1 $$W_{out} = \left \lfloor{\frac{W_{in} - K_W + 2*pad}{stride}+1}\right \rfloor$$
0003e334 Github Links:
0003e343 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/conv_op.h
0003e38d - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/conv_op.cc
0003e3d8 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/conv_pool_op_base.h

0003e4cb     ["Y"],

0003e4e4     pad=1,
0003e4ef     stride=2
0003e4ff // Create X: (N,C,H,W)
0003e516 data = np.random.randn(1,1,8,8).astype(np.float32)
0003e549 print("Data shape: ",data.shape)
0003e56b // Create W: (M,C,Kh,Kw)
0003e584 filters = np.random.randn(3,1,5,5).astype(np.float32)
0003e5ba print("Filter shape: ",filters.shape)
0003e5e1 // Create b: M
0003e5f0 bias = np.array([1.,1.,1.]).astype(np.float32)
0003e61f print("Bias shape: ",bias.shape)
0003e641 // Put the inputs into the workspace
0003e666 workspace.FeedBlob("X", data)
0003e684 workspace.FeedBlob("filter", filters)
0003e6aa workspace.FeedBlob("bias", bias)
0003e6cc // Run the operator
0003e6e0 workspace.RunOperatorOnce(op)
0003e6fe print("Y:\n", workspace.FetchBlob("Y"))

00040a25 NSt6__ndk110__function6__funcIZN6caffe223AveragePoolDocGeneratorEPKcE3$_0NS_9allocatorIS5_EEFvRNS2_8OpSchemaEEEE
00040a97 consumes an input blob and applies average pooling across the blob according
00040ae4 to kernel sizes, stride sizes, pad lengths and dilation. Average pooling consists
00040b36 of taking the average value of a subset of the input tensor according to the kernel
00040b8a size and downsampling the data into the output blob for further processing. The
00040bda `brew` module has a wrapper for this operator for use in a `ModelHelper` object.
00040c2c Pooling layers reduce the spatial dimensionality of the input blob. Each of the
00040c7c output blob's dimensions will reduce according to:
00040cb0 $$dim_{out}=\frac{dim_{in}-kernel+2*pad}{stride}+1$$
00040ce6 Github Links:
00040cf5 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/pool_op.h
00040d3f - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/pool_op.cc
00040d8a - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/conv_pool_op_base.h
00040de0 <details>
00040deb <summary> <b>Example</b> </summary>
00040e10 **Code**
00040e1e workspace.ResetWorkspace()
00040e3a op = core.CreateOperator(
00040e54     "AveragePool",
00040e67     ["X"],

000411ea ZN6caffe223AveragePoolDocGeneratorEPKcE3$_0
00041216 NSt6__ndk110__function6__funcIZN6caffe219MaxPoolDocGeneratorEPKcE3$_0NS_9allocatorIS5_EEFvRNS2_8OpSchemaEEEE
00041284 consumes an input blob and applies max pooling across the blob according to
000412d0 kernel sizes, stride sizes, pad lengths and dilation. Max pooling consists of
0004131e taking the maximum value of a subset of the input tensor according to the kernel
0004136f size and downsampling the data into the output blob for further processing. The
000413bf `brew` module has a wrapper for this operator for use in a `ModelHelper` object.
00041411 Pooling layers reduce the spatial dimensionality of the input blob. Each of the
00041461 output blob's dimensions will reduce according to:
00041495 $$dim_{out}=\frac{dim_{in}-kernel+2*pad}{stride}+1$$
000414cb Github Links:
000414da - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/pool_op.h
00041524 - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/pool_op.cc
0004156f - https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/caffe2/operators/conv_pool_op_base.h
000415c4 <details>
000415cf <summary> <b>Example</b> </summary>

000452ef [Only NHWC order is supported now]Note that other parameters, such as the stride and
00045344 kernel size, or the pads' sizes in each direction are not necessary for input
00045392 because they are provided by the ConvPoolOpBase operator. Various dimension
000453de checks are done implicitly, and the sizes are specified in the Input docs for
0004542c this operator. As is expected, the filter is convolved with a subset of the
00045478 image and the bias is added; this is done throughout the image data and the
000454c4 output is computed. As a side note on the implementation layout:
00045505 conv_op_impl.h is the templated implementation of the conv_op.h file, which is
00045554 why they are separate files.
